Already prone to overcommitment, she reacted by saying yes to every new project offer, expanding her workload beyond what she could realistically manage, while also worrying about her irregular income and overdue taxes. Over the next three months, her previously comforting routines around gardening, walking, and museum visits were increasingly displaced by frantic efforts to \"fix\" her financial situation through ambitious new commissions and speculative proposals, straining her organization and leading to late payments and confused interactions with clients. Her sister and a close neighbor encouraged her to seek help after they noticed she was talking rapidly about improbable financial opportunities, neglecting basic paperwork, and insisting she had discovered a \"sure way\" to secure her retirement, prompting her to present for psychiatric care when she realized her money management and work commitments were spiraling out of control.\n- Chief Complaint: “I keep taking on more and more projects and plans at once, and I can’t seem to slow myself down even though everything in my life is starting to fall apart.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Lately I’ve been completely convinced that the IRS and one of my old clients are quietly coordinating to sabotage me—like the delayed payments, the lost checks, even the audit notice are all part of a plan to push me into bankruptcy because they know I’m onto some big, valuable ideas, and no matter what my sister says I can’t shake the feeling that it’s intentional, not coincidence or clerical error.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Most days I’m either buzzing with this almost giddy excitement about all my “brilliant” projects or snapping at people for the smallest interruption—like I’ll be raving to my neighbor about how I’m going to triple my income, and then if she asks a simple question I’ll suddenly feel enraged, like she’s trying to block me or slow me down.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I wake up before dawn already pacing around the apartment, jumping from my desk to the kitchen to my studio corner, sketching, answering emails, reorganizing files, and I just can’t sit still long enough to finish any one thing before I’m compelled to start three more.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I keep telling my sister that I’m finally operating at the level I was meant to, that I can single-handedly revive my whole career and even “redefine illustration education” with these workshops I’m planning, and I honestly feel baffled and a little insulted when she suggests I might be overestimating what one person can do.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): When I get going about my new commissions and this “retirement plan” I’ve cooked up, I talk so fast and jump from idea to idea that people have to interrupt me to ask me to slow down, but I feel this pressure in my chest to keep explaining before the thoughts slip away, so it’s really hard to stop or let anyone else get a word in.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll sit down to invoice a client and then the ping of an email, a bird outside, or even a color in a magazine will pull me off into another direction—suddenly I’m redesigning my website, then sketching a new series, then reorganizing my brushes, and an hour later I realize I never finished the invoice I started.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I’ve been saying yes to every commission, pitching new series to galleries, drafting online course outlines, and even sketching a children’s book all at once, to the point where my calendar is a wall of overlapping deadlines and I’m constantly juggling late nights and frantic emails just to keep anything from completely collapsing.\n- S041 (Painful_Consequence_Behavior): I’ve started putting big expenses on my nearly maxed-out credit cards for supplies and “investments” in my brand, and I even put down a nonrefundable deposit on a pricey studio space I can’t really afford because I was so sure these grand plans would pay off, even though a more rational part of me knows I’m gambling with money I don’t have.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode: That feeling that the IRS and my old client are secretly plotting against me only started when I ramped up into this overcharged, sleepless, idea-filled state a few months ago, and in the calmer stretches of my life before this I never had those kinds of suspicions or any experiences like hearing or seeing things.\n- functional_impairment: Because I’ve overbooked myself and chased these grand schemes, I’ve missed multiple deadlines, confused invoices, fallen behind on my taxes, and strained relationships with clients and my sister to the point where I’m genuinely worried I could lose my main sources of income and support.\n- duration: All of this—the nonstop projects, the racing plans, and the paranoid feeling about my finances—has been building and spiraling for about three months, ever since those long-term contracts ended and the adjunct course was canceled.\n\nRules:\n- If the Complaint: “I keep taking on more and more projects and plans at once, and I can’t seem to slow myself down even though everything in my life is starting to fall apart.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Lately I’ve been completely convinced that the IRS and one of my old clients are quietly coordinating to sabotage me—like the delayed payments, the lost checks, even the audit notice are all part of a plan to push me into bankruptcy because they know I’m onto some big, valuable ideas, and no matter what my sister says I can’t shake the feeling that it’s intentional, not coincidence or clerical error.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Most days I’m either buzzing with this almost giddy excitement about all my “brilliant” projects or snapping at people for the smallest interruption—like I’ll be raving to my neighbor about how I’m going to triple my income, and then if she asks a simple question I’ll suddenly feel enraged, like she’s trying to block me or slow me down.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I wake up before dawn already pacing around the apartment, jumping from my desk to the kitchen to my studio corner, sketching, answering emails, reorganizing files, and I just can’t sit still long enough to finish any one thing before I’m compelled to start three more.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I keep telling my sister that I’m finally operating at the level I was meant to, that I can single-handedly revive my whole career and even “redefine illustration education” with these workshops I’m planning, and I honestly feel baffled and a little insulted when she suggests I might be overestimating what one person can do.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): When I get going about my new commissions and this “retirement plan” I’ve cooked up, I talk so fast and jump from idea to idea that people have to interrupt me to ask me to slow down, but I feel this pressure in my chest to keep explaining before the thoughts slip away, so it’s really hard to stop or let anyone else get a word in.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll sit down to invoice a client and then the ping of an email, a bird outside, or even a color in a magazine will pull me off into another direction—suddenly I’m redesigning my website, then sketching a new series, then reorganizing my brushes, and an hour later I realize I never finished the invoice I started.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I’ve been saying yes to every commission, pitching new series to galleries, drafting online course outlines, and even sketching a children’s book all at once, to the point where my calendar is a wall of overlapping deadlines and I’m constantly juggling late nights and frantic emails just to keep anything from completely collapsing.\n- S041 (Painful_Consequence_Behavior): I’ve started putting big expenses on my nearly maxed-out credit cards for supplies and “investments” in my brand, and I even put down a nonrefundable deposit on a pricey studio space I can’t really afford because I was so sure these grand plans would pay off, even though a more rational part of me knows I’m gambling with money I don’t have.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode: That feeling that the IRS and my old client are secretly plotting against me only started when I ramped up into this overcharged, sleepless, idea-filled state a few months ago, and in the calmer stretches of my life before this I never had those kinds of suspicions or any experiences like hearing or seeing things.\n- functional_impairment: Because I’ve overbooked myself and chased these grand schemes, I’ve missed multiple deadlines, confused invoices, fallen behind on my taxes, and strained relationships with clients and my sister to the point where I’m genuinely worried I could lose my main sources of income and support.\n- duration: All of this—the nonstop projects, the racing plans, and the paranoid feeling about my finances—has been building and spiraling for about three months, ever since those long-term contracts ended and the adjunct course was canceled.\n\nRules:\n- If the But if anyone interrupts me or questions my plans, I get incredibly irritable and just snap at them.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this pattern of intense excitement, taking on multiple projects, and irritability, is it a persistent trait or has it been episodic with clear periods of normal functioning in between?\n\nPatient: This has been building and spiraling for about three months, ever since I unexpectedly lost my illustration contracts and a teaching course. It is definitely episodic, as I've had much calmer stretches of life before this where I functioned normally without any of these intense feelings.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of abnormally depressed mood or loss of interest in activities that you once enjoyed, either before or after these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced periods of deep depression or lost interest in the things I love. My main issue has been this recent overwhelming speed, overcommitting to everything, and the stress of it all rather than feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization, due to these episodes of increased activity and excitement, or have they been manageable despite causing some problems?\n\nPatient: It definitely isn't manageable anymore, though I haven't been hospitalized. I’ve missed critical deadlines, confused my client invoices, fallen behind on my taxes, and heavily strained my relationship with my sister. I feel like things are completely spiraling out of control and risking my entire livelihood.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychotic symptoms, such as hallucinations or delusions, during these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity?\n\nPatient: Yes, lately I've been completely convinced that the IRS and one of my old clients are secretly coordinating to sabotage me and push me into bankruptcy. This paranoid feeling only happens when I'm in these high-energy states, and I've never experienced things like hearing or seeing things that aren't there."

My main issue has been this recent overwhelming speed, overcommitting to everything, and the stress of it all rather than feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization, due to these episodes of increased activity and excitement, or have they been manageable despite causing some problems?\n\nPatient: It definitely isn't manageable anymore, though I haven't been hospitalized. I’ve missed critical deadlines, confused my client invoices, fallen behind on my taxes, and heavily strained my relationship with my sister. I feel like things are completely spiraling out of control and risking my entire livelihood.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychotic symptoms, such as hallucinations or delusions, during these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity?\n\nPatient: Yes, lately I've been completely convinced that the IRS and one of my old clients are secretly coordinating to sabotage me and push me into bankruptcy. This paranoid feeling only happens when I'm in these high-energy states, and I've never experienced things like hearing or seeing things that aren't there.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how long these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity typically last, and do you have any periods where you return to a normal mood and functioning in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This current high-energy state has been building and spiraling continuously for about three months, ever since my contracts were canceled. Before this episode, I had long, calm stretches in my life where my mood and functioning returned completely to normal. During those normal times, I didn't experience any of these racing thoughts or paranoid feelings."

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. While I definitely had calmer, normal periods in my life before this, these past three months have just been a constant, building spiral. I haven't had any moments of normal functioning since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your intense excitement and increased activity lasted less than a week, or have all your episodes, including this current one, lasted several weeks to months?"

hard to stop or let anyone else get a word in.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll sit down to invoice a client and then the ping of an email, a bird outside, or even a color in a magazine will pull point where my calendar is a wall of overlapping deadlines and I’m constantly juggling late nights and frantic emails just to keep anything from completely collapsing.\n- S041 ramped up into this overcharged, sleepless, idea-filled state a few months ago, and in the calmer stretches of my life before this I never had those kinds of suspicions or any experiences like hearing Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I keep taking on more and more projects and plans at once, and I can’t seem to slow myself down even though everything in my life is starting to fall apart. My sister and neighbor really encouraged me to come in because my commitments and money management are just spiraling out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense excitement, euphoria, or a sense of invincibility when you take on these multiple projects and plans?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—most days I have this buzzing, giddy excitement about all my projects, and I feel like I'm finally operating at the high level I was always meant to. But if anyone interrupts me or questions my plans, I get incredibly irritable and just snap at them.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this pattern of intense excitement, taking on multiple projects, and irritability, is it a persistent trait or has it been episodic with clear periods of normal functioning in between?\n\nPatient: This has been building and spiraling for about three months, ever since I unexpectedly lost my illustration contracts and a teaching course. It is definitely episodic, as I've had much calmer stretches of life before this where I functioned normally without any of these intense feelings.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of abnormally depressed mood or loss of interest in activities that you once enjoyed, either before or after these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced periods of deep depression or lost interest in the things I love. My main issue has been this recent overwhelming speed, overcommitting to everything, and the stress of it all rather than feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, or required hospitalization, due to these episodes of increased activity and excitement, or have they been manageable despite causing some problems?\n\nPatient: It definitely isn't manageable anymore, though I haven't been hospitalized. I’ve missed critical deadlines, confused my client invoices, fallen behind on my taxes, and heavily strained my relationship with my sister. I feel like things are completely spiraling out of control and risking my entire livelihood.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychotic symptoms, such as hallucinations or delusions, during these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity?\n\nPatient: Yes, lately I've been completely convinced that the IRS and one of my old clients are secretly coordinating to sabotage me and push me into bankruptcy. This paranoid feeling only happens when I'm in these high-energy states, and I've never experienced things like hearing or seeing things that aren't there.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how long these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity typically last, and do you have any periods where you return to a normal mood and functioning in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This current high-energy state has been building and spiraling continuously for about three months, ever since my contracts were canceled. Before this episode, I had long, calm stretches in my life where my mood and functioning returned completely to normal. During those normal times, I didn't experience any of these racing thoughts or paranoid feelings.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many of these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity you've experienced in the past year, and do you notice any pattern or trigger that leads to the start or end of these episodes?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about other episodes over the past year, as I've been so completely focused on what's been happening these last three months. But this current spiral was definitely triggered when I suddenly lost my illustration contracts and my adjunct teaching course was canceled.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this current episode has lasted about three months, but can you think of any previous episodes in your life where you experienced similar intense excitement, increased activity, and then a return to normal functioning, and if so, how long those episodes typically lasted?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, but I don't think I've ever experienced an episode like this in the past. While I've had normal ups and downs, my life has mostly consisted of much calmer stretches before this current period. I certainly haven't had previous episodes of this intensity or duration.\n\nDoctor: Can you clarify if you have had any previous episodes similar to this three-month episode, and if so, were they shorter, or is this the first time you've experienced such a prolonged period of intense excitement and increased activity?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, and I haven't thought much about whether I've had similar episodes in the past. This is definitely the first time I've felt this completely overwhelmed and out of control. Right now, I'm just trying to focus on how to handle my current situation.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to recall if you've had any shorter periods, maybe a few days or a week, where you felt similarly excited, energetic, and took on a lot, but then returned to normal, before this three-month episode started?\n\nPatient: I don't recall having any shorter episodes like this in the past. While I've had calmer periods of life, this level of intense energy and taking on so many projects is entirely new and hasn't happened in shorter bursts before.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned this current episode has lasted about three months, but to clarify, have you had any distinct periods of normal functioning in between episodes of intense excitement and increased activity, or has this been a continuous state since it started?\n\nPatient: Since those contracts ended three months ago, this state has been completely continuous and non-stop without any breaks or normal days. While I definitely had calmer, normal periods in my life before this, these past three months have just been a constant, building spiral. I haven't had any moments of normal functioning since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your intense excitement and increased activity lasted less than a week, or have all your episodes, including this current one, lasted several weeks to months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any short periods like that lasting less than a week. This current episode has been going on continuously for about three months, ever since my contracts were canceled. It has just been a sustained, building spiral during this entire time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this episode has lasted about three months, but to confirm, is this the longest episode you've experienced, and have you had any episodes in the past that were significantly shorter or longer than this one?"

This paranoid feeling only happens when I'm in these high-energy states, and I've never experienced things like hearing or seeing things that aren't there.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how long these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity typically last, and do you have any periods where you return to a normal mood and functioning in between these episodes?\n\nPatient: This current high-energy state has been building and spiraling continuously for about three months, ever since my contracts were canceled. Before this episode, I had long, calm stretches in my life where my mood and functioning returned completely to normal. During those normal times, I didn't experience any of these racing thoughts or paranoid feelings.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many of these episodes of intense excitement and increased activity you've experienced in the past year, and do you notice any pattern or trigger that leads to the start or end of these episodes?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about other episodes over the past year, as I've been so completely focused on what's been happening these last three months. But this current spiral was definitely triggered when I suddenly lost my illustration contracts and my adjunct teaching course was canceled.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this current episode has lasted about three months, but can you think of any previous episodes in your life where you experienced similar intense excitement, increased activity, and then a return to normal functioning, and if so, how long those episodes typically lasted?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, but I don't think I've ever experienced an episode like this in the past. While I've had normal ups and downs, my life has mostly consisted of much calmer stretches before this current period. I certainly haven't had previous episodes of this intensity or duration.\n\nDoctor: Can you clarify if you have had any previous episodes similar to this three-month episode, and if so, were they shorter, or is this the first time you've experienced such a prolonged period of intense excitement and increased activity?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, and I haven't thought much about whether I've had similar episodes in the past. This is definitely the first time I've felt this completely overwhelmed and out of control. Right now, I'm just trying to focus on how to handle my current situation.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to recall if you've had any shorter periods, maybe a few days or a week, where you felt similarly excited, energetic, and took on a lot, but then returned to normal, before this three-month episode started?\n\nPatient: I don't recall having any shorter episodes like this in the past. While I've had calmer periods of life, this level of intense energy and taking on so many projects is entirely new and hasn't happened in shorter bursts before.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned this current episode has lasted about three months, but to clarify, have you had any distinct periods of normal functioning in between episodes of intense excitement and increased activity, or has this been a continuous state since it started?\n\nPatient: Since those contracts ended three months ago, this state has been completely continuous and non-stop without any breaks or normal days. While I definitely had calmer, normal periods in my life before this, these past three months have just been a constant, building spiral. I haven't had any moments of normal functioning since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your intense excitement and increased activity lasted less than a week, or have all your episodes, including this current one, lasted several weeks to months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any short periods like that lasting less than a week. This current episode has been going on continuously for about three months, ever since my contracts were canceled. It has just been a sustained, building spiral during this entire time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this episode has lasted about three months, but to confirm, is this the longest episode you've experienced, and have you had any episodes in the past that were significantly shorter or longer than this one?\n\nPatient: Yes, this current episode has been going on for about three months now. I'm really not sure and haven't kept track of whether I've had similar episodes in the past that were shorter or longer. This is just the most severe and overwhelming period I can ever remember experiencing."
  }

Over the next three months, her previously comforting routines around gardening, walking, and museum visits were increasingly displaced by frantic efforts to \"fix\" her financial situation through ambitious new commissions and speculative proposals, straining her organization and leading to late payments and confused interactions with clients. intentional, not coincidence or clerical error.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Most days I’m either buzzing with this almost giddy excitement about all my “brilliant” projects or snapping at people for the smallest interruption—like I’ll be raving to my neighbor about how I’m going to triple my income, and then if she asks a simple question I’ll suddenly feel enraged, like she’s trying to reorganizing files, and I just can’t sit still long enough to finish any one thing before I’m compelled to start three more.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I keep telling my sister that I’m finally little insulted when she suggests I might be overestimating what one person can do.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): When I get going about my new commissions and this “retirement plan” I’ve cooked up, I talk so fast and jump from idea to idea that people have to interrupt me to ask me to slow down, but I feel this pressure in my chest to keep explaining before the thoughts slip away, so it’s really hard to stop or let anyone else get a word in.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll sit down to invoice a client and then the ping of an email, a bird outside, or even a color in a magazine will pull me off into another direction—suddenly I’m redesigning my website, then sketching a new series, then reorganizing my brushes, and an hour later I realize I never finished the invoice I started.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I’ve been saying yes to every commission, pitching new series to galleries, drafting online course outlines, and even sketching a children’s book all at once, to the point where my calendar is a wall of overlapping deadlines and I’m constantly juggling late nights and frantic emails just to keep anything from completely collapsing.\n- S041 pricey studio space I can’t really afford because I was so sure these grand plans would pay off, even though a more rational part of me knows I’m gambling with money I don’t have.\n▸ Additional ramped up into this overcharged, sleepless, idea-filled state a few months ago, and in the calmer stretches of my life before this I never had those kinds of suspicions or any experiences like hearing Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nYes, absolutely—most days I have this buzzing, giddy excitement about all my projects, and I feel like I'm finally operating at the high level I was always meant to. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
